This needs no new booking machinery: an event type\\\\n-- already carries length, notice, buffers, window and price, and every layer\\\\n-- (page, edge function, slot policy, claim transaction) is already keyed by\\\\n-- event slug. A one-off link IS a booking_event_types row with a random slug and\\\\n-- single_use set.\\\\n--\\\\n-- Two things stood in the way and are fixed here.\\\\n--\\\\n-- 1. `single_session_bookings` still carried CHECK constraints from the day\\\\n--    there was exactly one sellable thing: `end_at = start_at + 70 minutes` and\\\\n--    `amount_cents = 4500`. Those made a custom-length or custom-price event\\\\n--    impossible to insert. They also mean the already-seeded 30-minute free\\\\n--    `consultation` event cannot currently be booked at all -- its insert\\\\n--    violates the 70-minute check. Replacing them with a trigger that reads the\\\\n--    event's own length fixes both.\\\\n--\\\\n-- 2. Nothing enforced single use. The same trigger does it, under a per-slug\\\\n--    advisory lock so two people clicking the same link at the same moment\\\\n--    cannot both get through.\\\\n\\\\n-- --------------------------------------------------------------------------\\\\n-- The link itself\\\\n-- --------------------------------------------------------------------------\\\\nALTER TABLE public.booking_event_types\\\\n  ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS single_use boolean NOT NULL DEFAULT false,\\\\n  ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS pinned_start_at timestamptz,\\\\n  ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS note text;\\\\n\\\\nCOMMENT ON COLUMN public.booking_event_types.single_use IS\\\\n  'A one-off link: once a booking against this slug is held or paid, the slug is spent and no further booking may be claimed.';\\\\nCOMMENT ON COLUMN public.booking_event_types.pinned_start_at IS\\\\n  'One exact start time this link sells. When set, the link offers only this time and the notice, window and weekly-hours rules do not apply -- Joshua already decided this time is fine. Double-booking is still refused by the claim transaction.';\\\\nCOMMENT ON COLUMN public.booking_event_types.note IS\\\\n  'Admin-only reminder of who a one-off link was made for. Never sent to the browser.';\\\\n\\\\n-- A pinned time is a one-off idea: it names a single instant, so it can only be\\\\n-- sold once.\\\\nALTER TABLE public.booking_event_types\\\\n  DROP CONSTRAINT IF EXISTS booking_event_types_pinned_is_single_use_chk;\\\\nALTER TABLE public.booking_event_types\\\\n  ADD CONSTRAINT booking_event_types_pinned_is_single_use_chk\\\\n  CHECK (pinned_start_at IS NULL OR single_use);\\\\n\\\\n-- --------------------------------------------------------------------------\\\\n-- Duration and price stop being constants\\\\n-- --------------------------------------------------------------------------\\\\nALTER TABLE public.single_session_bookings\\\\n  DROP CONSTRAINT IF EXISTS single_session_duration_chk,\\\\n  DROP CONSTRAINT IF EXISTS single_session_amount_chk;\\\\n\\\\nALTER TABLE public.single_session_bookings\\\\n  DROP CONSTRAINT IF EXISTS single_session_amount_nonnegative_chk;\\\\nALTER TABLE public.single_session_bookings\\\\n  ADD CONSTRAINT single_session_amount_nonnegative_chk\\\\n  CHECK (amount_cents IS NULL OR amount_cents >= 0);\\\\n\\\\n-- --------------------------------------------------------------------------\\\\n-- What the dropped CHECKs used to say, said against the event instead\\\\n-- --------------------------------------------------------------------------\\\\nCREATE OR REPLACE FUNCTION public.enforce_booking_event_shape()\\\\nRETURNS trigger\\\\nLANGUAGE plpgsql\\\\nSECURITY DEFINER\\\\nSET search_path = public\\\\nAS $$\\\\nDECLARE\\\\n  _event record;\\\\n  _spent integer;\\\\nBEGIN\\\\n  SELECT * INTO _event\\\\n  FROM public.booking_event_types\\\\n  WHERE slug = NEW.event_slug;\\\\n\\\\n  IF NOT FOUND THEN\\\\n    RAISE EXCEPTION 'unknown_event' USING ERRCODE = 'P0400';\\\\n  END IF;\\\\n\\\\n  -- The length is the event's, not the caller's. This is the old 70-minute\\\\n  -- CHECK, generalised: a consultation is 30 minutes and a one-off link is\\\\n  -- whatever it was minted as.\\\\n  IF NEW.end_at <> NEW.start_at + make_interval(mins => _event.length_minutes) THEN\\\\n    RAISE EXCEPTION 'duration_mismatch' USING ERRCODE = 'P0400';\\\\n  END IF;\\\\n\\\\n  IF _event.single_use THEN\\\\n    -- Serialise every claim against this one link, so a simultaneous pair of\\\\n    -- clicks cannot both read \\\\\\\"unspent\\\\\\\" and both insert. Taken before the read,\\\\n    -- released at transaction end.\\\\n    PERFORM pg_advisory_xact_lock(hashtext('one-off-booking-link:' || NEW.event_slug));\\\\n\\\\n    -- Spent means a booking exists that is live or was paid. A hold holds the\\\\n    -- link (so nobody else can start one while a checkout is open); expiring or\\\\n    -- abandoning that hold releases it again. A cancelled or refunded booking\\\\n    -- stays spent -- the session was sold once, and a replacement gets a\\\\n    -- replacement link.\\\\n    SELECT count(*) INTO _spent\\\\n    FROM public.single_session_bookings b\\\\n    WHERE b.event_slug = NEW.event_slug\\\\n      AND b.status NOT IN ('expired', 'abandoned')\\\\n      -- A retry of the same request is the same booking, not a second one.\\\\n      AND b.client_idempotency_key <> NEW.client_idempotency_key;\\\\n\\\\n    IF _spent > 0 THEN\\\\n      RAISE EXCEPTION 'link_already_used' USING ERRCODE = 'P0410';\\\\n    END IF;\\\\n  END IF;\\\\n\\\\n  RETURN NEW;\\\\nEND;\\\\n$$;\\\\n\\\\nDROP TRIGGER IF EXISTS enforce_booking_event_shape ON public.single_session_bookings;\\\\nCREATE TRIGGER enforce_booking_event_shape\\\\n  BEFORE INSERT ON public.single_session_bookings\\\\n  FOR EACH ROW EXECUTE FUNCTION public.enforce_booking_event_shape();\\\\n\\\\nREVOKE ALL ON FUNCTION public.enforce_booking_event_shape() FROM public, anon, authenticated;\\\\n\\\\n-- --------------------------------------------------------------------------\\\\n-- Is a one-off link still open? Read by the booking page before it offers times.\\\\n-- --------------------------------------------------------------------------\\\\nCREATE OR REPLACE FUNCTION public.booking_event_is_spent(_event_slug text)\\\\nRETURNS boolean\\\\nLANGUAGE sql\\\\nSTABLE\\\\nSECURITY DEFINER\\\\nSET search_path = public\\\\nAS $$\\\\n  SELECT EXISTS (\\\\n    SELECT 1\\\\n    FROM public.booking_event_types t\\\\n    JOIN public.single_session_bookings b ON b.event_slug = t.slug\\\\n    WHERE t.slug = _event_slug\\\\n      AND t.single_use\\\\n      AND b.status NOT IN ('expired', 'abandoned')\\\\n  );\\\\n$$;\\\\n\\\\nREVOKE ALL ON FUNCTION public.booking_event_is_spent(text) FROM public, anon, authenticated;\\\\nGRANT EXECUTE ON FUNCTION public.booking_event_is_spent(text) TO service_role;\\\\n\\\\n-- --------------------------------------------------------------------------\\\\n-- A pinned link sells one instant, and only that instant\\\\n--\\\\n-- Everything this function normally asks -- is it far enough out, is it inside\\\\n-- the rolling window, does it fall inside the weekly hours and on the slot grid\\\\n-- -- exists to decide whether a time is one Joshua would agree to. A pinned\\\\n-- link is that agreement, made in advance and by hand: he picked the time and\\\\n-- sent it to one person. So the questions are answered, and the only thing left\\\\n-- to check is that the caller is asking for the time on the link.\\\\n--\\\\n-- This does NOT relax the double-booking check. That lives in\\\\n-- claim_single_session_slot, runs inside the booking transaction, and still\\\\n-- refuses a slot that overlaps another booking or a calendar event.\\\\n--\\\\n-- Links without a pinned time keep the ordinary policy, reading their own\\\\n-- minimum_notice_minutes and window_business_days -- which is how a short-notice\\\\n-- link is made: mint it with a notice of zero.\\\\n-- --------------------------------------------------------------------------\\\\nCREATE OR REPLACE FUNCTION public.is_single_session_slot_bookable(\\\\n  _start_at timestamptz,\\\\n  _now timestamptz DEFAULT now(),\\\\n  _event_slug text DEFAULT 'single-session'\\\\n) RETURNS boolean\\\\nLANGUAGE plpgsql STABLE SET search_path = public AS $$\\\\nDECLARE\\\\n  _tz text := COALESCE(\\\\n    (SELECT timezone FROM public.booking_availability_config WHERE id = 1),\\\\n    'America/Toronto'\\\\n  );\\\\n  _event public.booking_event_types%ROWTYPE;\\\\n  _length integer;\\\\n  _pitch integer;\\\\n  _local_start timestamp;\\\\n  _local_date date;\\\\n  _local_time time;\\\\nBEGIN\\\\n  SELECT * INTO _event FROM public.booking_event_types WHERE slug = _event_slug AND active;\\\\n  IF NOT FOUND THEN RETURN false; END IF;\\\\n\\\\n  IF _start_at IS NULL THEN RETURN false; END IF;\\\\n\\\\n  -- The pinned instant, and nothing else. Still refuse to sell one in the past.\\\\n  IF _event.pinned_start_at IS NOT NULL THEN\\\\n    RETURN _start_at = _event.pinned_start_at AND _start_at > _now;\\\\n  END IF;\\\\n\\\\n  _length := _event.length_minutes;\\\\n  -- No explicit interval means slots start every length_minutes, which is what\\\\n  -- Cal.com does and what produces the session's 1:00 / 2:10 / 3:20 chain.\\\\n  _pitch := COALESCE(_event.slot_interval_minutes, _event.length_minutes);\\\\n\\\\n  IF _start_at < _now + make_interval(mins => _event.minimum_notice_minutes) THEN\\\\n    RETURN false;\\\\n  END IF;\\\\n\\\\n  _local_start := _start_at AT TIME ZONE _tz;\\\\n  _local_date := _local_start::date;\\\\n  _local_time := _local_start::time;\\\\n\\\\n  IF _local_date < (_now AT TIME ZONE _tz)::date\\\\n     OR _local_date > public.single_session_booking_window_end(_now, _event_slug) THEN\\\\n    RETURN false;\\\\n  END IF;\\\\n\\\\n  RETURN EXISTS (\\\\n    SELECT 1\\\\n    FROM public.booking_availability a\\\\n    WHERE a.day_of_week = extract(dow FROM _local_date)::integer\\\\n      AND _local_time >= a.start_time\\\\n      AND _local_time + make_interval(mins => _length) <= a.end_time\\\\n      AND mod(\\\\n        floor(extract(epoch FROM (_local_time - a.start_time)) / 60)::integer,\\\\n        _pitch\\\\n      ) = 0\\\\n  );\\\\nEXCEPTION WHEN OTHERS THEN\\\\n  -- Invalid timezone/configuration is not permission to sell a slot.\\\\n  RETURN false;\\\\nEND;\\\\n$$;\\\\n\\\\nREVOKE ALL ON FUNCTION public.is_single_session_slot_bookable(timestamptz, timestamptz, text) FROM public, anon, authenticated;\\\\nGRANT EXECUTE ON FUNCTION public.is_single_session_slot_bookable(timestamptz, timestamptz, text) TO service_role;\\\"}\">\n</lov-tool-use>\n\n<lov-tool-use id=\"functions.dispatch:2_58611e80\" name=\"supabase--deploy_edge_functions\" data=\"{\\\"function_names\\\": [\\\"single-session\\\"]}\">\n</lov-tool-use>\n\n<lov-tool-use id=\"thinking\" name=\"lov-think\" data=\"Both operations succeeded. === frontmatter sample ===
---
name: guided-drill-assignment
description: Use this whenever Joshua asks to look at a student's transcript, choose an appropriate LSAT U guided drill, verify the drill works, assign it through the platform, or confirm that the student received a clear notification. This skill covers the full workflow from transcript review to guided drill recommendation, platform/browser QA, assignment, and notification verification.
---

# Guided Drill Assignment

Use this skill to turn student performance context into a verified guided drill assignment in LSAT U.

The operating standard is: do not merely recommend a drill. Choose the right drill from the student's recent transcript/context, verify the drill actually launches and is wired correctly, assign it through the platform, then verify the student's notification surface.

## Inputs

Expect one or more of:

- A student name or shorthand, such as `C`, `C. Alexander`, or another current student.
- A request to inspect a recent transcript, chat export, meeting note, student page, or platform record.
- A request to find an appropriate guided drill.
- A request to assign a drill and make sure the student is notified.

If the student identity is ambiguous and cannot be safely inferred from local context, ask one concise clarifying question before assigning anything.

## Required Context

For LSAT U vault work, follow the vault operating order before making a recommendation:

{"file_path": "C:\\Users\\Joshu\\.claude\\skills\\booking-link\\SKILL.md", "content": "---\nname: booking-link\ndescription: Use whenever Joshua wants a one-off booking link for LSAT U — \"make me a link for Dakota at 7:15 tomorrow\", \"send her a link to book tonight\", \"one-off link, $60\", \"short notice link\", \"I need a link she can schedule herself with\". Mints a single-use URL that sells exactly one session at a price you set, optionally pinned to one exact time, and closes after she books. Also covers listing and revoking existing links.\n---\n\n# One-off booking links\n\nA URL Joshua sends to one person. She opens it, books one session, pays, and\ngets the Google Meet invite from the ordinary booking flow. Then the link\ncloses.\n\n## Authorization\n\nMinting a link is pre-authorized. Joshua asking for a link means make it now —\ndo not ask permission, ask only for the facts you actually need.\n\n**Never send the link anywhere.** Print it and hand it to Joshua. He decides who\ngets it. A link is a Bearer [REDACTED]: whoever holds it can book that slot.\n\n## What you need before running\n\nOnly two things are required, and one has a default:\n\n- **Price** in dollars. Required — links always charge, and the standard rate is\n  $45 but a given person may pay something else. Ask if it was not said.\n- **Who it is for.** Used only for the admin-side note, never in the URL. If\n  Joshua did not say, use a short description.\n\nEverything else is optional. Ask for nothing you were not given.\n\n## Running it\n\nFrom `~/ai-os/projects/lsatu`:\n\n```bash\nnpm run booking:link -- --for \"Dakota\" --price 45 --at \"2026-09-10 19:15\"\n```\n\nTimes in `--at` are wall-clock in Joshua's booking timezone (`America/Toronto`),\nformat `YYYY-MM-DD HH:mm`, 24-hour. Convert \"7:15pm tomorrow\" yourself — resolve\nrelative dates against today before running, and never pass a relative word\nthrough.\n\nIt prints the URL, the price, the time, and how many times the link can sell.\nGive Joshua the URL exactly as printed.\n\n### The flags\n\n| Flag | Default | What it does |\n|---|---|---|\n| `--for` | — | Who the link is for. Note only, never in the URL. |\n| `--price` | — | Dollars. Required. |\n| `--at` | none | Pin one exact time. See below. |\n| `--minutes` | 70 | Session length. |\n| `--notice` | 1440, or 0 with `--at` | Minimum notice in minutes. `0` means \"bookable right now\". |\n| `--window` | 6 | How many business days ahead the picker reaches. |\n| `--label` | \"LSAT Session\" | The title she sees on the page. |\n| `--note` | auto | Admin reminder of who and why. |\n\n### Pinned versus picker\n\n**`--at` pins one time.** She sees only that time and confirms it. The minimum\nnotice, the rolling window and the weekly hours are all skipped, because Joshua\nchoosing the time *is* that decision. This is the right shape for \"I have a gap\nat 7:15 tonight, let her take it.\"\n\n**Without `--at` she gets a calendar** of open times, still inside the weekly\nhours. Use `--notice 0` for a short-notice link she can book today, and\n`--window` to control how far ahead it reaches.\n\nDouble-booking is never skipped either way. A pinned time that clashes with\nsomething already on the calendar is refused at mint time — the script deletes\nthe link and tells you what it hit — and refused again inside the booking\ntransaction.\n\n## Listing and revoking\n\n```bash\nnpm run booking:link -- --list\nnpm run booking:link -- --revoke <slug>\n```\n\n`--list` shows every one-off link with its state: `open`, `used`, or `revoked`.\nRevoking is immediate and the URL stops resolving.\n\n## Reading the outcome honestly\n\n- **\"clashes with something already on your calendar\"** — the time is taken. No\n  link was created. Tell Joshua what he asked for is not free and offer to pick\n  another time; do not silently mint a different one.\n- **\"would have no open times at all\"** — a picker link whose window and weekly\n  hours produce nothing. Usually `--window` is too small or the hours are empty.\n- **A time in the past** is refused. If Joshua asks for a time that has already\n  gone by today, say so rather than rolling it to tomorrow on your own.\n\n## How it works, so you can debug it\n\nA one-off link is a row in `booking_event_types` with a random 10-character slug\nand `single_use` set. That is the same table defining the ordinary session and\nthe consultation, and every layer beneath — the page, the `single-session` edge\nfunction, the SQL slot policy, the claim transaction — is already keyed by event\nslug. So the link books through exactly the code a normal session books through.\nThere is no second booking path to keep in sync.\n\nSingle use is enforced by the `enforce_booking_event_shape` trigger under a\nper-slug advisory lock, so two people opening the same link at the same moment\ncannot both get through. A hold holds the link; letting the checkout expire\nreleases it; a cancelled or refunded booking stays spent, and a replacement\nneeds a fresh link.\n\nRelevant files:\n\n- `scripts/booking-link.ts` — the CLI\n- `src/pages/OneOffBooking.tsx` and the `/book/x/:slug` route in `src/App.tsx`\n- `supabase/migrations/20260908120000_one_off_booking_links.sql`\n- `supabase/functions/single-session/index.ts`\n\n## Credentials\n\n`LSATPREP_ADMIN_EMAIL` / `LSATPREP_ADMIN_PASSWORD` from `~/ai-os/.env`, which is\ngit-ignored. This repo's `.env` is tracked in git — never put a credential\nthere.\n"}

---
title: Cal.com stuck bookings
type: runbook
created: 2026-09-08
updated: 2026-09-08
tags: [runbook, calcom, payments, scheduling]
---

# Cal.com stuck bookings

When a student says scheduling or payment "isn't working", this is almost always the cause.

## What happens

`session-link` charges Stripe **on booking** ($45 USD). The flow is: pick a slot, fill the form,
press **Pay to book**, land on `cal.com/payment/<uuid>` with Stripe card fields. That page holds
the slot for **30 minutes only**:

> "This slot is held for you for 29:54 — after that, someone else may book it."

Miss that window and three things are true at once:

1. The payment link is dead. The uuid is not stored on the booking and API v2 exposes no
   payments endpoint, so **it cannot be recovered or re-sent**.
2. `cal.com/booking/<uid>` shows "awaiting payment" with only *Add to calendar* and *Cancel*.
   **There is no Pay button** on any unpaid booking. Verified against a control booking.
3. The dead booking **keeps holding the slot**, hiding it from everyone else. Cal.com sends
   no host notification, so it is invisible from this side.

## The check

```
node scripts/calcom-pending-sweep.mjs           # report
node scripts/calcom-pending-sweep.mjs --cancel  # release future slots
```

Run it when a student reports trouble, and periodically — these accumulate and silently eat
availability. Past-dated pending bookings cannot be cancelled by cal.com and block nothing;
leave them.

## The only fix

Cancel the stale booking and have the student **book again**. That issues a fresh 30-minute
link. There is no way to revive the old one. The 30-minute deadline is not settable through the
API — only in the cal.com dashboard under the paid event's settings.

## Config as of 2026-09-08

- Schedule 681247 "Working Hours", **America/Toronto**. Mon 14:00–20:00, Tue/Thu 13:00–19:30,
  Wed 13:00–19:00, Fri 13:00–19:30, Sat 14:00–17:00. Sunday closed.
- Event type 4753558, 70 min, no slot interval, so slots step 70 minutes from each window start
  and fill around existing bookings.
- Booking window widened 2026-09-08 from **6 business days** to **45 calendar days**. The old
  setting meant students saw barely one week ahead and at most one Saturday, which is what made
  weekend booking so hard.
- Date override: **2026-09-19 20:00–21:10 ET** for [[../students/fernande-charleus/fernande-charleus]].

## History

2026-09-08 — Whitney Wilson lost two bookings this way (Sep 10 and Sep 12) within four minutes

#!/usr/bin/env node
// Find cal.com bookings stuck at "pending" — a student filled the booking form but
// never finished Stripe checkout. The payment link holds the slot for 30 minutes and
// then dies; it CANNOT be re-sent (the uuid is not stored on the booking and API v2
// has no payments endpoint). Meanwhile the dead booking keeps holding the slot.
//
// cal.com sends no host notification for these, so they are invisible without this check.
//
//   node scripts/calcom-pending-sweep.mjs           # report
//   node scripts/calcom-pending-sweep.mjs --cancel  # also release future slots
//
// Needs CALCOM_API_KEY in .env. API v2 only — v1 is decommissioned.

import { readFileSync } from "node:fs";
import { fileURLToPath } from "node:url";
import { dirname, join } from "node:path";

const root = join(dirname(fileURLToPath(import.meta.url)), "..");
const env = Object.fromEntries(
  readFileSync(join(root, ".env"), "utf8")
    .split("\n")
    .filter((l) => l.trim() && !l.trim().startsWith("#") && l.includes("="))
    .map((l) => [l.slice(0, l.indexOf("=")).trim(), l.slice(l.indexOf("=") + 1).trim()])
);

const KEY = process.env.CALCOM_API_KEY || env.CALCOM_API_KEY;
if (!KEY) { console.error("CALCOM_API_KEY missing from .env"); process.exit(1); }

const api = (path, init = {}) =>
  fetch(`https://api.cal.com/v2${path}`, {
    ...init,
    headers: {
      Authorization: [REDACTED] ${KEY}`,
      "cal-api-version": "2024-08-13",
      "Content-Type": "application/json",
      ...init.headers,
    },
  }).then((r) => r.json());

const ET = (iso) =>
  new Date(iso).toLocaleString("en-US", {
    timeZone: "America/Toronto", year: "numeric", month: "short",
    day: "numeric", hour: "numeric", minute: "2-digit",
  });

{"file_path": "C:\\Users\\Joshu\\dev\\ai-os\\projects\\lsatu\\docs\\implementation-plans\\CALCOM-EXIT-SPEC.md", "content": "# Leaving Cal.com — spec and plan\n\n**Written 2026-09-09.** Goal: every booking Joshua takes happens on LSAT U, and the\nCal.com account is closed.\n\nEvery number in the \"Cal.com today\" tables was read live from the Cal.com API v2\non 2026-09-09, and every number in the \"LSAT U today\" tables was read from the\nproduction database on the same day. Nothing here is remembered or assumed. Where\nsomething is unverified it says so.\n\n---\n\n## The headline\n\nThis is a smaller job than it looks. Cal.com has twelve event types, and **ten of\nthem are the same session sold to one person at their own price and length.**\nLSAT U already models that as data rather than as twelve hand-made pages, so most\nof the migration is rows, not features.\n\nWhat is genuinely missing is five things, and none is large:\n\n1. Per-event schedules (the consultation runs on different hours).\n2. Date overrides (a one-off evening opened for one student).\n3. A booking window measured in calendar days, not business days.\n4. Emailing a link from the platform.\n5. Reminders, and student-initiated reschedule.\n\n---\n\n## Ground truth: Cal.com today\n\n### The twelve event types\n\nAll twelve place their meeting through the Google Meet integration. None uses\nseats, recurrence, or requires-confirmation, so none of that needs building.\n\n| Slug | Title | Min | Price | Buffers | Notice | Public |\n|---|---|---:|---:|---|---:|---|\n| `consultation` | Consultation | 30 | free | 30 / 30 | 24 h | **yes** |\n| `session-link` | LSAT Session | 70 | $45.00 | 0 / 0 | 24 h | no |\n| `sabrina` | Sabrina | 90 | $60.00 | 15 / 15 | 24 h | no |\n| `rr` | Reduced link | 75 | $56.00 | 15 / 15 | 24 h | no |\n| `booking-reduced-rate` | Booking (Reduced Rate) | 90 | $52.50 | 15 / 15 | 10 h | no |\n| `session` | Session | 60 | $40.00 | 0 / 0 | 6 h | no |\n| `reduced-rate` | Reduced rate | 70 | $40.00 | 0 / 0 | 48 h | no |\n| `tutoring` | Dakota | 60 | $55.00 | 10 / 10 | 24 h | no |\n| `c-reduced-rate` | Reduced Rate | 70 | $35.00 | 10 / 10 | 48 h | no |\n| `canadian-scheduling` | Canadian Scheduling 🇨🇦 | 70 | $61.00 | 10 / 10 | 48 h | no |\n| `complimentary-session` | Complimentary session | 45 | free | 0 / 0 | 2 h | no |\n| `lsat` | LSAT | 65 | $60.00 | 0 / 0 | 2 h | no |\n\nSix distinct lengths are in use: 30, 45, 60, 65, 70, 75, 90.\n\n### The two schedules\n\n**\"Working Hours\"** (id 681247, default, America/Toronto) — Mon 14:00–20:00,\nTue 13:00–19:30, Wed 13:00–19:00, Thu 13:00–19:30, Fri 13:00–19:30,\nSat 14:00–17:00. Sunday closed. Carries date overrides.\n\n**\"Consultation\"** (id 696906, America/Toronto) — **all seven days, 13:30–20:00.**\n\nThis second schedule is the thing most likely to be missed. The consultation is\ndeliberately easier to book than a session: wider hours, and Sundays open.\n\n### Other live behaviour\n\n- Payment is taken **on booking**. The Stripe page holds the slot 30 minutes and\n  then dies unrecoverably, stranding the slot. See [[CALCOM-STUCK-BOOKINGS]].\n- Booking window was widened 2026-09-08 from 6 business days to **45 calendar\n  days**, because students could see barely a week and at most one Saturday.\n- At least one date override is live: 2026-09-19, 20:00–21:10 ET, for one student.\n\n---\n\n## Ground truth: LSAT U today\n\n### Weekly hours — already correct\n\n`booking_availability`, timezone `America/Toronto`, matches Cal.com's Working\nHours exactly, day for day:\n\n| Day | LSAT U | Cal.com Working Hours |\n|---|---|---|\n| Mon | 14:00–20:00 | 14:00–20:00 |\n| Tue | 13:00–19:30 | 13:00–19:30 |\n| Wed | 13:00–19:00 | 13:00–19:00 |\n| Thu | 13:00–19:30 | 13:00–19:30 |\n| Fri | 13:00–19:30 | 13:00–19:30 |\n| Sat | 14:00–17:00 | 14:00–17:00 |\n| Sun | closed | closed |\n\n### Event types\n\n| Slug | Min | Price | Buffers | Notice | Window |\n|---|---:|---:|---|---:|---|\n| `single-session` | 70 | $45.00 | 0 / 0 | 24 h | 6 business days |\n| `consultation` | 30 | free | 30 / 30 | 24 h | 5 business days |\n\n### What already works, and should not be rebuilt\n\n- Slot generation, weekly hours, buffers, per-event notice and slot pitch.\n- Google Calendar: live free/busy, event creation, Meet link, mirrored events.\n- Stripe checkout, holds, expiry sweeper, refunds, cancellation with tokens.\n- Booking confirmation and cancellation/refund emails.\n- Per-person pricing by email (`booking_price_overrides`).\n- One-off single-use links, optionally pinned to an exact time (shipped\n  2026-09-08, `booking-link` skill).\n- A hold model that is **better than Cal.com's**: LSAT U holds the slot, expires\n  it, and sweeps it back. The stuck-booking failure that loses Joshua sessions\n  today is a Cal.com defect that does not exist here.\n\n---\n\n## The gaps\n\nSeven items. Each says what is missing and what it costs.\n\n### 1. Per-event schedules — blocks the consultation\n\nCal.com's consultation runs on its own hours (7 days, 13:30–20:00). LSAT U has\nexactly one weekly-hours table, so a consultation on LSAT U would inherit\nsession hours and lose Sundays and its earlier start.\n\nNeeds: a `booking_schedules` table, `booking_availability.schedule_id`, and a\n`booking_event_types.schedule_id`, defaulting to the current rows so nothing\nmoves. Two SQL functions read availability and must take the schedule.\n\n### 2. Date overrides — a live feature with a booking already on it\n\nCal.com has at least one override open right now. LSAT U has no concept of one,\nso migrating today would silently drop it.\n\nNeeds: a `booking_date_overrides` table (date, optional window, or closed), read\nby the slot policy and the generator. A closed override must also refuse a\nbooking that is already pinned to that day.\n\n### 3. Booking window unit mismatch\n\nLSAT U counts **6 business days**; Cal.com now uses **45 calendar days**. Ported\nas-is, students would again see about a week — the exact problem Joshua fixed on\nCal.com the day before.\n\nNeeds: `window_calendar_days` alongside, or replacing, `window_business_days`.\nRecommend replacing: business days were a workaround, and calendar days are what\nthe product actually wants.\n\n### 4. Emailing a link\n\nAsked for directly. Today `booking-link` prints a URL to the terminal.\n\nNeeds: an `--email` flag that renders a short template through the existing\n`send-transactional-email` function. Everything underneath exists; this is a\ntemplate plus a flag. **The email must be a draft-and-confirm step, not a silent\nsend** — house rule.\n\n### 5. Reminders — not built\n\nCal.com sends them. LSAT U sends nothing between confirmation and the session.\nVerified: no reminder code exists anywhere in the repo.\n\nNeeds: a scheduled sweep over confirmed bookings plus a reminder template.\nNon-trivial but well-bounded, and it reuses the outbox worker pattern the single\nsession already has.\n\n### 6. Student-initiated reschedule — not built for single sessions\n\n`student-booking` has reschedule for the pass flow; the single-session path has\ncancel only. Cal.com lets an attendee reschedule from the confirmation email.\n\nNeeds: a `reschedule` action re-running the slot claim under the existing cancel\ntoken, moving the Google event rather than making a second one.\n\n### 7. Ten personal event types to move\n\nEach is a person with their own length and price. LSAT U can express this today\nas a `booking_event_types` row per student with `single_use = false` — the same\nshape Cal.com uses, minus the hand-made page. `booking_price_overrides` covers\nprice but not length, so per-person **length** is what makes a row necessary.\n\nNeeds: no new schema. A `--personal` mode on the CLI, and ten rows.\n\n---\n\n## Decisions needed from Joshua\n\nNothing below is a technical unknown; each is a product call that changes what\ngets built.\n\n1. **Booking window:** replace business days with calendar days, and set it to 45\n   to match Cal.com? *(Recommend yes.)*\n2. **Personal link slugs:** random and unguessable, or readable like\n   `/book/x/dakota`? Readable is friendlier and is what Cal.com does; random\n   means the URL alone is the credential. *(Recommend readable for standing\n   student links, random for one-offs.)*\n3. **Reminders:** wanted at cutover, or after? If wanted, at what offsets — Cal.com's\n   defaults are 24 h and 1 h.\n4. **Reschedule:** may a student reschedule themselves, or does that stay with\n   Joshua? Today, on LSAT U, it is Joshua's.\n5. **Existing future Cal.com bookings:** migrate them into LSAT U, or let them\n   run out on Cal.com and stop taking new ones there?\n6. **The consultation is the public front door.** It is the only unhidden Cal.com\n   event. Moving it changes what a stranger from the website meets, so it should\n   move last and be watched.\n\n---\n\n## Plan\n\nEach phase ends in something verifiable. ## assistant

The spec is at [docs/implementation-plans/CALCOM-EXIT-SPEC.md](projects/lsatu/docs/implementation-plans/CALCOM-EXIT-SPEC.md). I pulled both systems live rather than working from memory — your Cal.com account through the API, and the LSAT U production database.

**The finding that matters:** this is a smaller job than it looks. You have twelve Cal.com event types, and **ten of them are the same session sold to one person at their own price and length.** LSAT U already models that as data. Most of the migration is rows, not features.

Your weekly hours already match Cal.com exactly, day for day. That surprised me and it's the largest single piece of parity, already done.

**Five real gaps**, and one of them is a trap:

- **Your consultation runs on a second schedule** — id 696906, all seven days, 13:30–20:00, deliberately easier to book than a session. LSAT U has one hours table. Move the consultation today and it silently inherits session hours, losing Sundays and the earlier start.
- **You have a live date override** (Sep 19, 20:00–21:10, one student). LSAT U has no concept of one. It would vanish in the move.
- **Window unit mismatch.** LSAT U counts 6 business days; you widened Cal.com to 45 calendar days *the day before yesterday*, because students saw barely a week. Port LSAT U as-is and you reintroduce the problem you just fixed.
- **Reminders don't exist** in LSAT U. I checked the whole repo — nothing between confirmation and the session.
- **Emailing the link** is a template plus a flag; everything under it is built.

**Where LSAT U is already better:** your hold model. Cal.com's payment page dies after 30 minutes and the dead booking keeps eating the slot — the thing that cost you Whitney's two sessions. LSAT U holds, expires, and sweeps it back. That defect doesn't exist here, and the spec flags it as something not to regress.

**Six decisions are yours**, listed in the doc. The two I'd settle first: whether the window becomes 45 calendar days (I recommend yes), and whether standing student links get readable slugs like `/book/x/dakota` or stay random.

One thing I'd build before any of it: a diff script that pulls Cal.com's slots and LSAT U's for the same event and reports disagreement. That's the acceptance test for the entire migration, and it's cheap.

Also worth knowing: Phase 0 accidentally fixed your consultation. It couldn't be booked *at all* on LSAT U — a 30-minute booking violated a leftover 70-minute constraint. Unverified live, so treat that as likely rather than proven.

Want me to start on the diff script, or work through the six decisions first?
